## Society of Stalwart Adventurers

The Society of Stalwart Adventurers was an exclusive club in Suzail, Cormyr, founded to bring together renowned world travelers and adventurers.

Membership
To be invited to join, a prospective member must have achieved some noteworthy feat and have it recognized by the society's committee. However, over the course of its thousand-year history, the society was infiltrated by wealthy individuals of Cormyr. Despite providing financial support for the expeditions of the legitimate members, these individuals, referred to by Artus Cimber as "Warts" rather than Stalwarts, diminished the prestige of the society.

History
The Society of Stalwart Adventurers was founded around the 2 century DR by Dhalmass Rayburton and four other members. However, Dhalmas later confessed to Artus Cimber that he'd just allowed the use of his name to the original founders of the society.

Notable Members
 Dhalmass Rayburton, a member of House Rayburton, and member of the barae of Mezro in Chult.
 Artus Cimber, the famous Cormyrean adventurer who for decades carried the burden of bearing the legendary Ring of Winter.
 Hydel Pontifax, a devoted follower of Mystra and a companion of Artus Cimber.
 Theron Silvermace, a Harper and a member of the Silvermace noble family of Cormyr.
 Philyra, a druid and an accomplished adventurer.
 Lady Elynna, the president of the society as of 1362 DR, who had a pet leopard.
 Uther, the butler of the society in the 1360s, who looked like an Abyssal denizen because of a spell cast by Danilo Thann

## Sorrow's Tear

The Sorrow's Tear was a unique ghostly dagger created out of a ghost's tear by Elytharra Dinnesmore of Targos in the early 14 century DR.

Powers
This dagger was a weightless ethereal silver blade. The weapon's magic came from the ghostly's tear and the feelings of sorrow and relief that it contained. When the blade landed a stab, it had a chance of transferring the ghost's suffering anguish onto the target. The weapon possessed a +2 enchantment that was especially deadly to incorporeal undead creatures.

History
In the, the goblin slayers of Targos may have aided Cahl-Hyred, the innkeeper of the Weeping Widow Inn, to aid the establishment's namesake. The weeping ghost was the widow Veira who waited for her husband, who perished in Maer Dualdon in years past. When she was shown a piece of Veira's Gaze, the ghost gifted adventurers a single ghostly tear as a thank you. Later, enchantress Elytharra Dinnesmore offered to use the tear's magic and shape it into a magical spectral blade.

## Soul Coins: A Treatise

Soul Coins: A Treatise was an academic work outlining the nature and specifics of soul coins, published by the scholars of Candlekeep.

Description
Soul Coins was published solely for informational purposes only and not intended as an endorsement of soul harvesting.

Content
Research for Soul Coins was conducted in Avernus the uppermost plane of the Nine Hells.

The book described the nature and function of soul coins. It outlined their usage among the infernal denizens of the Hells, namely in commerce, as fuel for war machines, and the creation of certain magic items.

It detailed the infernal contract signed certain half-elf warlock, that bound his soul to the creation of a soul coin upon the time of his death.

History
As of the, a copy of this book was left in the Emerald Grove of the Western Heartlands.

## Soraevora Aeravand

Soraevora Aeravand was an aged sun elf divine soul of Angharradh, the Queen of Arvandor, from the elven island of Evermeet, active in the late 14 century DR.

Description
In the late 14 century DR, Soraevora appeared aged. As a sun elf, it meant that she had lived many hundreds of years. She had a thin face, graying hair and golden gray eyes, and bronze skin. She carried herself with dignity that advertised the woman's wisdom, while her eyes betrayed her ferocity and dedication to her faith and quest.

Possessions
Soraevora wielded a unique enchanted spear, sacred to her goddess. Soraevora's chainmail was one of the finest creations of Evermeet's smiths.

History

In the, Soraevora Aeravand had been alive for many centuries. She received visions from the Queen of Arvandor, her deity, that foretold a dire future for the elves and other races of Faerûn. A dire threat of an interloper deity – Zehir, and his Forktongue Cultists loomed over the nation of Samarach, Chult, and even the Sword Coast. She was shown the faces of heroes who would stand against the yuan-ti and their deity. Soraevora followed the divine guidance to the Crossroad Keep in Northwest Faerûn.

At the keep, she met Khelgar Ironfist, who was put in charge by Lord Neverember while the illustrious Knight-Commander was missing. Initially, Khelgar Ironfist attempted to send the elf woman away, yet she stayed adamant. When Graycloaks attempted to escort her out, she knocked them out with the spear's haft. Khelgar had no choice but to let her wait for the adventurers at the keep. Soraevora did not expect to receive a respectful welcome from a dwarf, but Khelgar once traveled with a pious elf and understood the strange woman's drive. Soon after, she met the subjects of her dreams – a group of adventurers who allied with the merchant prince Sa'Sani of the Mercantile House.

Soraevora pledged her service and her spear to the adventurers in order to stop the dark god from swallowing the Sword Cost. After the heroes traveled to the Temple of World Serpent from where the Forktongue Cultists operated, and defeated Zehir's avatar, Soraevora Aeravand returned to the Crossroad Keep to walk its halls dance more. Now, her journey was complete and shortly after, the favored soul departed the Sword Coast to return to Evermeet.

## Soraevora's spear

Soraevora's spear was a unique polearm, sacred to the goddess Angharradh, The Triune Goddess from the elven island of Evermeet.

Powers
Soraevora's spear was enhanced with Angharradh's divine magic, granting it a +2 enchantment to damage that guided the spear toward its target. The spear dealt piercing and additional divine damage upon each strike. Soraevora's spear's unique nature only made it usable by goodly elf favored souls.

History
This spear's existence predated the 14 century DR, likely by many centuries. The weapon was entrusted to a favored soul of Angharradh – the sun elf named Soraevora Aeravand of Evermeet. In the, Soraevora's deity sent her visions of doom and the elf embarked on the journey to the Sword Coast North to stop Zehir, a yuan-ti interloper deity. Soraevora's spear accompanied her on the perilous quest.

## Softpox

Softpox was a disease found only in the Underdark.

Acquisition
The disease could be contracted through physical contact with an infected individual.

Effects
Softpox caused the skin to become soft and swollen, rendering it tender and painful. This weakening of the skin caused sufferers to repeatedly experience severe injury for as long as they were ill. Infected creatures with naturally tough skins or hides found their natural defenses winnowing down to nothing, leaving them likewise suffering injuries from the disease.

Treatment
It was virtually impossible for a normal person to fight off the disease naturally, but the injuries inflicted by the disease could be healed or recovered from on a day-by-day basis.

## Soraevora's chain

Soraevora's chain was a unique suit of chainmail that was sacred to the goddess Angharradh, The Triune Goddess, from the elven island of Evermeet.

Powers
This beautiful  chainmail was expertly crafted by elven artisans of Evermeet and tailored specifically to Soraevora Aeravand's body. The chainmail was decorated with a tabard that hid underneath a chain that held the holy symbol of Angharradh in front of the wearer's heart. The armor possessed a +2 protective enchantment, gave them limited spell resistance, and improved the wearer's reflexes. The magical divine nature of Soraevora's chain ensured that it could only be worn by a favored soul who was an elf and who was both good and chaotic in spirit.

History
This chainmail's existence predated the 14 century DR, likely by many centuries. The armor was worn a favored soul of Angharradh: the sun elf named Soraevora Aeravand of Evermeet. In the, Soraevora's deity sent her visions of doom and the elf embarked on a journey to the Sword Coast North to stop Zehir, a yuan-ti interloper deity. Soraevora's chain accompanied her on the perilous quest.

## Sorrow

Sorrow was a unique, enchanted glaive that gradually twisted the soul of the wielder over time.

Powers
Every time the wielder of Sorrow wielded another creature with its place, their psyche became a little more fractured and damaged. It was said that every drop of blood tarnished their soul, a process that would continue until nothing else remained.

The wielder could use Sorrow to magically ensnare their foe in a tangle of vines.

History
It was said to have once belonged to the "shade" of an individual, or perhaps a member of the shade species. It was taken from that individual around the time Ketheric Thorm was defeated, and the shadow curse spread outward from Moonrise Towers.

As of the, Sorrow remained stored in a secret vault within the depths of the Emerald Grove.

## Sorghum

Sorghum was a grain cultivated as food during summer months. It could also be used as a sweetener.

Availability
Places in the Realms where sorghum was known to be eaten included:
 In Chult, where the Tabaxi tribe grew Sorghum in the Agricultural Quarter of Mezro.
 In Kara-Tur, where sorghum was one of several grains—allong with barley and millet—seen as a poor person's alternative to rice. It was often eaten either as a thick gruel or pounded into cakes. Sorghum was cultivated in Ma' Yuan, such as by the monks of the Strong Claw monastery near Yen Ching.

History
During the invasion of Shou Lung by the Tuigan in the, a decisive battle in the Ma' Yuan Province came to be known as the Battle of the Sorghum Field.

In the mid-to-late 14 century DR, sorghum-derived sweeteners could be purchased for 3 silver pieces per .

## Sode garami

A sode garami  was a weapon unique to the continent of Kara-Tur. It was also known as the sleeve entangler.

Description
The sode garami was a highly specialized weapon employed for the purpose of capturing and ensnaring an opponent without inflicting significant harm. Typically designed as a set comprising a pole  in length and crossbar adorned with numerous spikes and hooks.

Upon a successful strike, it ensnared and latched onto the target's clothing. When the sode garami was used for capturing, it inflicted no direct damage. However, forceful strikes could also be executed with the weapon, resulting in both the described effect and the associated damage. This weapon found frequent usage among palace guards and city constables.

## Sode

Sode was a type of armor used in Kara-Tur.

Description
The sode was a protective armor that shielded the wearer's shoulder. Typically square or rectangular in shape, it measured approximately . The sode was securely attached to other armor pieces, particularly the corselet, using cords. As a result, it could not be worn independently and necessitated the presence of the do-maru, hara-ate, haramaki, or haramaki-do.

During battles, it was not uncommon to tie a piece of cloth known as the sode jiruchi over the sode. This cloth was often dyed or painted with a distinctive emblem or badge that identified the wearer.

Soda ash could be bought from Aurora's Emporium for 3 sp per, intended for use in a wizard's laboratory.

 The animate fire spell used some soda ash, charcoal, and sulfur.

 A carbonate of soda such as natron was used in mummification as a preservative.

## Soot

Soot was a common material substance of the Realms, a kind of impure carbon similar to lampblack, coke and graphite.

Description
Soot was a deep black, fine powder, similar to ash.

Availability
Soot was produced as a residue wherever fuel was burned, such as candles, torches, lamps, fireplaces, forges, and wildfires.

Aurora's Emporium sold soot for 2 cp per, intended for the arcane uses of wizards.

Usages
Soot was a common ingredient in inks and dyes for giving a strong black pigment.

Soot could be used to add carbon to an alloying process, such as making steel.

Spell Components
Soot was an easily available magical material component, but it could still take time and effort to find a supply with exactly the right properties required.

 Cacodemon used a complex ritual with many ingredients including soot.
 Comprehend languages used a pinch of soot and a few grains of salt.
 Crawling darkness used a pinch of soot or pitch.
 Doomtide used a pinch of soot, among other ingredients.
 Friends could use a variety of powders and pigments, including soot.
 Greater creature of darkness used a pinch of soot or pitch and other ingredients.
 Melt used a pinch of soot and rock salt.
 Plague used a pinch of soot or another black powder, and a scrap of human skin.
 Smokescreen used a pinch of soot.

## Sound bubble

Sound bubble was an alteration spell used by wizards and spellcasting bards that created a sound barrier around an area of the caster's choosing. It was often used to either enhance a bardic performance's effect and quality or for similar situations as the silence spell was used for.

Effects
Casting this spell would cause an invisible bubble to be created, either on an area or centered around the caster. In the latter case, the bubble would move with the caster. A stationary bubble had a radius of, while a mobile bubble had a radius of . Any sound made within this bubble was not audible to creatures outside of it and any sounds made outside of the bubble could not be heard by those within it.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, casting this spell required a material component. This could take the form of either a soap bubble or a blown egg shell.

Notable Users
 Maxamillion Darknote.

## South Road (Amn)

The South Road was an old, important road built in Amn that connected much of its frontiers.

Description
The length and remoteness of the road made it very difficult to upkeep. It was effectively a muddy, rutted trail that picked its way through the often rough and hilly terrain of Amn's lightly-populated hinterlands.

Geography
The South Road ran around the distant eastern and southern borders of Amn, curving hundreds of miles from Eshpurta, through the Tejarn Hills, and around to Eshpurta. The section of road between Eshpurta and Trollford was known as the Troll Road, and it was standard for travelers and caravans to receive an escort by the garrison at Eshpurta along this stretch.

Notable locations
 Eshpurta, the military capital of the eastern frontier.
 Trollford, the only safe crossing over the Amstel River.
 Hillfort Keshla, the easternmost Hillfort in the Tejarn Hills.
 Hillfort Torbold, the middle Hillfort.
 Hillfort Ishla, the westernmost Hillfort in the Small Teeth.
 Citadel of the Banner Raised Anew, a militant temple of Arvoreen.
 Zinner, a village on the Hyrzashyr.
 Gambiton, a small village.
 King's Arch, an ancient bridge across the River Esmel.
 Esmeltaran, the inland former capital of Amn.

History
The South Road was built during the Third Age of Calimshan ( to ) to link its distant northern garrisons and outposts across the Ralamnish Ridings, such as the Nine Sentinels. Earlier on, the South Road forked south from the Tejarn Hills into Tethyr to provide a fast direct route from Shoonach, though this section of road disappeared with time.

The road continued to be used through the 14 century by merchants and the Council Army, passing through the fallen realms of Meiritin and Cortryn that were absorbed into Amn.

## Soren Arnsfirth

Soren Arnsfirth was a Reghedmen human warrior from the Tribe of the Elk in the late 15 century DR.

Personality
Soren was a humble individual with no grasp of how spirits, gods, or magic function.

Description
Soren had sky-blue eyes. As of 1485 DR, he was one of the eldest warriors of the tribe.

Relationships
Soren had a daughter, Hedrun, who was gifted with then-unknown powers emerging and frightening the members of the Tribe of the Elk. Soren had no idea how to help his daughter. When she was cast out he didn't do anything, but years later he still hoped his daughter was alive.

Soren frequently sought the tribe shaman Mjenir's guidance, but each time, he left their discussions feeling frustrated because the shaman saw Hedrun as a threat to be controlled, rather than a child in need of support. Despite lacking the courage to openly challenge the shaman, Soren held a grudge against Mjenir for turning his back on the girl and expelling her from the tribe. His hesitation also revolved around the possibility that Mjenir's decision to banish Hedrun might have been justifiable. As a grieving father himself, Soren empathized with Mjenir's bitterness over the loss of his son Olaf as a result of Hedrun's uncontrollable powers.

History
Soren was both bewildered and fearful as his daughter Hedrun began to display peculiar abilities during her growth. He felt powerless as he observed the tribe turning its back on her, unsure of how to provide solace to his daughter or relate to her. After Mjenir's son Olaf was frozen to death because of Hedrun's powers manifesting, Soren couldn't prevent her banishment.

Around the, Soren started to contemplate the possibility that Hedrun might have survived, as the rumors of the Ice Witch sweeping through the Reghed Glacier began to spread. The only reason for his speculations was the father's hope that his daughter was still alive, and he didn't share these thoughts with the other members of the tribe. Soren backed Mjenir's idea of staying away from encounters with the witch as he was unwilling to witness his daughter's demise, no matter what she had become.

## Solid cloud

Solid cloud was an alteration spell, developed by the air elementalist Mortimer Lightfoot in the mid–14 century DR, that allowed a caster to make a single cloud tangible.

Effects
Upon having solid cloud cast on it, a cloud was capable of being walked on and supporting objects as if it were solid ground. When interacted with, a cloud had a cottony and soft touch. This effect was permanent.

Components
In addition to requiring somatic and verbal components, solid cloud required a material component, which took the form of the cloud that was to be used by the spell.

History
In the mid–14 century DR, Mortimer Lightfoot and a group of other rogue elementalists known as the Circle of Four used solid cloud, alongside Mortimer's other spell cloud shape, to create a sort of cloud palace that they used to imprison their kidnapped victims.

## Songblade

A songblade was an enchanted sword that could serve the role of a musical instrument when wielded in battle by a bard.

As of the late 15 century DR, the term "songblade" was sometimes used in places like Neverwinter to refer to a bard who specialized in swordfighting, often with a flamboyant or swashbuckling flair.

Description
While generally rapiers, songblades could be any kind of light or heavy blade. Intricate and magical patterns scored into the weapon created sweet, musical sounds as the blade moved through the air.

Powers
Songblades were magically sharp and strong weapons with which bards could perform their music, and thus use as implements for their spellcasting. Many also granted additional powers, such as magically enriching musical performances, providing bards with a deeper well of magical power, or unleashing loud sounds that could daze foes or rend their armor.

Because the blades required the movement of air across the blade to produce their music, songblades lost most of their utility in environments without air (such as under water or in a vacuum).

Types of Songblades

 Harsh songblade, which had the power to unleash a wailing note that stunned a bard's foes.
 Piercing songblade, which had the power to release a sound that could pierce through armor.

## Sour cream

Sour cream was a dairy product eaten in the Savage Frontier, the Vast, and Tethyr.

Availability
In the Western Heartlands and the city of Baldur's Gate, sour cream was often eaten with baguettes.

In the Savage Frontier, sour cream could be found in the town of Yartar. Beldar's Rest inn had fried potatoes on offer, flavored with a sauce of parsley, chives, and sour cream.

Further south, sour cream was consumed in Tethyr. Morgan's Inn in the city of Velen served highsunfeast of marinated herring topped with spur cream.

In the city of Ravens Bluff on the Dragon Reach, massive two-pound potatoes were filled with butter, cheese, and sour cream. This simple dish was worthy of the nobility's table, including Lauren Devillars's feasts.

During his visits to Earth to meet Mordenkainen and Rautheene in Ed Greenwood's estate, Elminster was known to have some of the Earth's sour cream with potato chips.

## Southfill River

The Southfill  River was a river in central Netheril that descended from the Lodestar Mountains and emptied into Hidden Lake.

Geography
The river originated in the southern Lodestar Mountains near Hatchet Mountain, wound across the plains, passed Canlespiere and through the Hidden Forest until it flowed into Hidden Lake.

History
The fertile lands along the river were home to the ogres of Chanth, who were assaulted and overtaken by expansionist Netherese settlers in . This began an eight-hundred-year period of war and skirmishes between the humans and ogres, culminating in the appearance of the ogre mage Storm. The north shore of the river was the site of the final great battle between a team of Netherese arcanists and Storm in, and though it was unclear if Storm was slain, he and his ogres never returned.

Somi and his brother entered Entharl Danthelon‎'s employ sometime in or before the . ## Sohar

Sohars were a type of ship commonly used by Calishites to ship goods.

Description
On average, from bow to stern these ships measured  in length, had a beam (width) of, and required a depth of at least  in order to float.

Sohars sported three masts and had a small forecastle. They could hold up to  of cargo and supplies, around as much as a cog, though sohars tended to be faster and more maneuverable than most cogs. Each sohar could man a crew of up twenty individuals, but the average number of crew one could find on a sohar would be around eight individuals. The bare minimum of individuals needed to sail a sohar were four.

History
Circa 1344 DR, a sohar being sailed by Calishite merchants sunk in the Sea of Fallen Stars, leaving a shipwreck.

By 1371 DR, sohars were becoming a more common sight in the Inner Sea.

Appendix
Background
Sohars first appeared in the AD&D accessory Of Ships and the Sea (1997). Every ship featured in that book is derived from a real historical type of ship, but the sohar is the one exception to this rule. In the text it is described as a "middle-eastern merchant ship" and that "adventurers will certainly encounter these ships in Arabian cultures". The only historical instance of a ship called "sohar" was a replica dhow built in 1980 for the British explorer Tim Severin. He named it in reference to the town of Sohar in Oman, which is commonly believed to be the birthplace of the legendary Sinbad the Sailor.

## Soldier's Monument

The tomb contained tablets with lore on gods of war, namely Tempus and the Red Knight, as well as a brief history of the tensions between Neverwinter and Luskan.

## Soldier's Memorial

Soldier's Memorial was an area within the Pauper's Section of Neverdeath Graveyard in Neverwinter where military veterans were interred.

History
In, a group of doomguides belonging to the Eternal Order of Kelemvor were lured into a trap by Thayan necromancers seeking to capture them for use in dark rituals. These captives were then kept within Soldier's Memorial.

Sometime in the 1480s DR, members of the Cult of the Dragon who followed Severin Silrajin sought the remains of Cruven Gantara. In their efforts to find him, they sought the remains of his close associate, Belast Messandar, who had been interred in Soldier's Memorial along with his closest allies having served in several campaigns. The Harpers coordinated efforts to thwart the Cult, and led by an agent named Boward, attempted to obscure the location of Messandar's final resting place.

In the, Thessamel Arunder's apprentice, Faeya, signed off on the sale of his tower to a cabal of merchants in exchange for 200,000 gp, a promise to remain unmolested, free firewood, and a dozen deer a year. ## Soggy Wren

Soggy Wren  was the pseudonym used by a notoriously alcoholic agent of the Harpers based out of Port Nyanzaru in Chult in the late 15 century DR. He was sometimes referred to simply as Soggy or simply as Wren.

Description
Wren had light skin and dark brown eyes. He was fat and of average height, and he dressed in dirty Chultan-style clothing. While Wren originally had potent sorcererous abilities, he had turned to ignoring them in favor of drinking.

Personality
He had an affable nature and was overly friendly, so much so that he could almost seem rude. He couldn't stand evil people or "party poopers". While he was generally comfortable just being himself and letting others think of him as they wished, he was prone to anger when he felt others had no faith in him. He had little interest in treasure and always strove to help his fellow Harpers and to do the morally right thing.

Wren was adept at acting under pressure and at resisting magical compulsion. He was also highly perceptive, able to draw connections between seemingly unrelated events and able to read people's body language. He was intimately familiar with the city of Port Nyanzaru and its darkest secrets, and was surprisingly knowledgeable of Chultan drugs, such as Wanderlost.

Despite all of his positive qualities, Soggy Wren was an extreme alcoholic, and this could make him unreliable and often difficult to understand because of his slurred speech.

Activities
He was the ranking member of the Harpers in Port Nyanzaru in the late 15 century DR, and so was the primary contact for other Harper agents. One of his duties, given the curse of undead in Chult at the time, was to collect the ashes of fallen Harpers.

Wren aspired to earn a transfer to somewhere with a more temperate climate, and could generally be found getting drunk on one of his "party tours" in Port Nyanzaru's Market Ward.

Relationships

Wren was on good terms with some native tribes of Chult, and had influence with the owners of most of the taverns in Port Nyanzaru because of his continued patronage. He had connections to bookies in the city, such as Mowbula Utakulu, and could arrange ship passage for his allies, such as aboard the Nathir with Captain Vaelara. Other Harpers active in Port Nyanzaru included Liandra Boldblade and Sirge Wintermelt, and the merchant prince Wakanga O'tamu was sympathic to the Harpers, even offering his villa as a safehouse. Later in life, Wren worked with fellow Harpers Zehira Deryan and Remallia Haventree.

He occasionally cooperated with representatives from other factions within Port Nyanzaru, such as when several of them elected to hire the mercenary Pozzanna Lordeaux to secure a stronghold within the Chultan jungle, when they rallied behind Hawkdriver Mazugulak to drive back an undead attack on the city, or when they came together under Alastar Bol to send agents to investigate undead forces stemming from the ruined city of Matolo. Professionally, he had working relationships with Nerissa from Mezro and the Triceratops Society through Diamssar. However, Diamssar was secretly a yuan-ti spy and had been deceiving Wren.

History
Early Life
Soggy Wren was a longtime Harper and had at one time been an adventurer and a sorcerer specializing in evocation magic. However, all that ended when he started drinking to cope with the losses and uncertainties of the adventuring lifestyle. This drinking led him to neglect his magical abilities and soon drove away many Harper acquaintances and friends, who felt that they could no longer trust him. He was often assigned to manage Harper safehouses in terrible or out-of-the way places, and was eventually transferred to Port Nyanzaru in Chult, where he disliked the oppressive heat.

In Chult
As of the events of the death curse around, Wren was a middle-aged man who could be seen most evenings at Kaya's House of Repose. He had been banned from many other drinking establishments in Port Nyanzaru, although he was also known to frequent the Thundering Lizard.

During this time, he juggled several missions as part of his calling to protect the people and thwart the death curse, including searching for the pieces of the Dreamer's Amulet that had recently appeared in the city (to investigate a potential connection between it and the death curse), investigating rumors that yuan-ti might try to infiltrate Port Nyanzaru and tamper with dinosaur races like Na N'buso's Haka, seeking alliances with Chultan tribes, and finding and spying on the "Ramshackle King".

When the Red Wizards of Thay began collecting fragments of the Soulmonger after the end of the death curse, Wren hatched a plot to capture and turn one of their operatives in order to learn their plans.

After Chult
Several years later when his hair had gone white, Wren finally confronted the fact that his drinking had created more problems than it had solved. He sobered up, regained his spellcasting abilities, and achieved peace with himself.

By this time, he had finally managed to get transferred out of the oppressive head of Chult and into the equally distasteful freezing cold of Icewind Dale. There, he was assigned alongside Zehira Deryan to the Harper safehouse in Xorvintroth, a buried city from Abeir in which the Red Wizards were active. While there, he was given terrible assignments, such as posing as a slave in order to provide support to other Harpers. When his fellow Harpers began to experience strange psychic effects caused by slivers of a key that could be used to release the Shadow in the Ice, a herald of Father Llymic trapped within the city, Wren seized a sliver and fled with it to protect them. He was subsequently captured by creatures of the Far Realm and tortured for information about the location of other slivers.

At some point, he devised a crossbow bolt designed to force a rolled-up portable hole into a compact bag of holding upon impact, causing an explosion that would drag nearby creatures and objects into the Astral Plane. He dubbed this his "weapon of mass devastation", or "WMD" for short. He was immensely proud of this invention, but refused to share the blueprints for its construction out of fear of causing an arms race.

## Sorrowsworn

A sorrowsworn was a class of shadow creatures considered to be manifestations of the Shadowfell itself. Some were embodiments of the different kinds of emotional suffering caused by the plane's effects on those who entered it: the angry, manifesting anger and conflict; the hungry, representing the unsated; the lonely, reflecting isolation; the lost, for anxiety, confusion, and fear; and the the wretched for, well, wretchedness. Others were fragments of death incarnate that preyed upon feelings of grief and loss. Three kinds were known: soulrippers, the stealthy assassins; reapers, the brutal slayers; and deathlords, their dread leaders.While both 4 and 5 editions relate sorrowsworn to the Shadowfell, they are very different in nature, concept, and naming (both are also different from the sorrowsworn demons of 3.5 edition). This article attempts to cover and reconcile both versions, but the reader should consider the version they prefer. For conciseness and clarity, in this article, the three forms of 4-edition sorrowsworn are referred to as "death sorrowsworn" and the five forms of 5-edition sorrowsworn are referred to as "emotion sorrowsworn", but these are not canon names and do not reflect a known in-universe distinction. Nevertheless, being related to the emotion of grief, the earlier sorrowsworn may be regarded as an advanced sixth form of the later, which supports their inclusion here.

Description
Each of the emotion sorrowsworn had forms reflecting the feelings they embodied, however strangely. For example, the angry sorrowsworn had hooks in place of hands and two heads that argued with each other when not directing their ire at another; the hungry sorrowsworn had gaping jaws and grasping claws; the lonely sorrowsworn had harpoons for hands that it could launch at others in order to drag them closer; and the lost sorrowsworn appeared terrified and desperate and had five spiked arms with which to grab onto others, while the wretched sorrowsorn were simply small and pitable, but with many fangs.

Death sorrowsworn bore a resemblance to undead and demonic creatures, but in truth were neither. Sorrowsworn reapers and deathlords dressed in robes.

Abilities
Angry sorrowsworn drew their sustenance from aggression and violence. They grew stronger as their targets fought back and harmed them, and became confused when they did not. Their two heads gave them good senses and made them hard to blind, charm, deafen, scare, stun, or knock unconscious.

Hungry sorrowsworn, with their insatiable appetites, simply tried to consume all they could get their claws on to: flesh, life, energy, even the screams of their victims. They grew more savage and deadly when they witnessed someone heal or otherwise gain vitality.

Lonely sorrowsworn longed for companionship and interaction. This somehow manifested in launchable harpoon arms that could skewer and ensnare other creatures up to  away and drag them closer into the lonely's sorrowful embrace. Creatures in close proximity, or worse, in such an embrace, suffered psychic harm. As misery loves company, the lonely had greater prowess when within  of at least two other creatures.

Similarly, the lost sorrowsworn also just needed a hug, in a vain effort to find comfort. However, its five arm spikes were not so comfortable for those it grabbed. Worse still, their minds suffered a deluge of fear and psychic harm, and the more their allies fought to free them, the more intense this effect grew.

Finally, the wretched sorrowsworn, like the hungry, subsisted on the life energy of their victims and took it in the form of flesh and fear. When one bit a victim, it latched on and drained life energy until forced off. They hunted in great packs.

All the emotion sorrowsworn were more resistant to harm whilst in areas of shadow or darkness.

Death sorrowsworn had a bleak visage that instilled fear in their foes, making it harder for them to fight back.

Combat

The various emotion sorrowsworn fought with their innate weapons, whether fanged bites, clawed hands, or strange hooks or harpoon arms. The hungry would grab on in order to bite instead, while the lonely and lost would grapple and hug to death. The wretched fought in large packs, becoming more dangerous when they and an ally flanked a target.

Death sorrowsworn fought with their claws or their scythes. Such weapons crumbled to dust with the sorrowsworn's passing. Soulrippers were stealthy and stalked and ambushed their victims; with a flutter of their wings, they would teleport, surprise, and grievously strike their targets, or just rush in and claw them apart. Focusing on one at a time, reapers made devastating scythe attacks; if a foe was knocked out or slain, the reaper was healed. Meanwhile, deathlords went for hit-and-run attacks, teleporting and passing through walls to reach any foe; as soon as they attacked, they were gone again, turned insubstantial as a shadow as they teleported away. Their mournful whispers dazed any being beside them and their scythes weakened their foes.

Behavior & Activities
Emotion sorrowsworn were compelled to inflict the suffering they embodied on those they encountered, and they wandered until they found a target for their manifest feelings. The emotion each represented indicated both its strengths and its vulnerabilities: succumbing to these feelings made the sorrowsworn stronger, while resisting them made them weaker and even repelled them. Those who died in the Shadowfell in the associated frame of mind could be transformed into sorrowsworn.

Death sorrowsworn consumed the feelings of great loss or sorrow that might linger in an area. Thus, they often appeared in the midst of great conflicts and were attracted to places where countless people had died, such as past battlefields and around graveyards, for these were places of old grief. They would lurk here until ready to collect fresh souls. They made their lairs in close-by ruins where they could feed on the despair that still hung over such places. Their lairs were bleak places, such as dank caves and thorn-filled pits. Small cavities in these places housed gruesome trophies and body parts, each with a grim tale behind it.

Death sorrowsworn, particularly those who dwelled in the Shadowfell, were servants of the Raven Queen. A few served other entities with power over death in a similar fashion. For the Raven Queen, they were guardians of the Shadowfell, custodians of decay, heralds of endings, and harbingers of doom. They were dispatched to kill great mortals who'd cheated death or her clutches and to take their souls. With innate knowledge of what their victims valued or regretted, they harassed them with whispers of previous failures and their coming fate. They also shielded the Raven Queen from the schemes of nightwalkers and fought against death giants who did not obey her. However, they were largely uninterested in this work and only rarely did they get involved in conflicts in the Shadowfell. While they did not look for foes, they were cruel and merciless in battle against nightwalkers, death giants, and others.

The Raven Queen created these death sorrowsworn from high-ranking shadar-kai and other servants by some means, granting them a form of the immortality they desired. A few of these sorrowsworn she appointed as her elite Raven Knights. Sorrowsworn were created at and guarded the Foundation of Loss in the Shadowfell.

Despite this, the majority of death sorrowsworn, even in the Shadowfell, were independent and worked to maintain the cycle of life, such as it was in the shadow. They dwelled in ruins, often those of defeated enemies like the death giants, and constructed no settlements or fortresses of their own, let alone cities.

Relations
Death sorrowsworn cooperated with one another, coming together in teams as needed to achieve their goals. They would ally with another creature if it was in their interest and did not set them back or cost them too much. Occasionally, they rode the shadowy fell wyverns.

Death sorrowsworn were served by shadowravens, ephemeral black birds of the Shadowfell, which they could command to gather in great flocks to harry their enemies with their razor-sharp necrotic talons. Scores of shadowravens guarded their lairs and some followed their masters to graveyards, battlefields, and other places of death wherein they could scavenge the dead. They served as ominous heralds of the sorrowsworn. Without sorrowsworn to command them, they behaved as simple crows.

History
In the Shadowfell, after the creation of the nightwalkers went against the natural order of things, the death sorrowsworn made war upon them. Their conflict left great swathes of the Shadowfell altered and damaged, but the sorrowsworn were implacable and the nightwalkers retreated into the deepest, darkest parts of the plane.

When Corellon battled Lolth, the Raven Queen joined his side. She lent her death sorrowsworn to the fight against Lolth's fallen elves and demons.

Some time after the Spellplague of the, a group of death sorrowsworn occupied a destroyed temple of Helm in a town that had fallen into a gorge in Chessenta, as it was a place where countless mortals had died in terror and pain. They considered it a sacred place and believed "something" conceived around 1439 DR sought to be born in the ruined temple and, as of 1479 DR, needed to gestate another 20 years more. But in that year the dracolich Alasklerbanbastos needed to perform a rite there, making use of the same power, and destroyed the shadowsworn. However, he found no trace of what they believed was growing there.

Members of the Dolindar family of wizards, who were trapped in the Shadowfell, rose after death as sorrowsworn within the Dolindar Tomb in the city of Evernight, by the 1490s DR. The family matriarch Kevetta became a lonely sorrowsworn due to her sense of isolation, while her children Evisha and Nolan became lost sorrowsworn.

## Soul of the Earth

The Soul of the Earth was an ancient and magical stone that allowed the user to scry on anyone who left behind an arrow or other projectile.

It was a polished piece of volcanic rock.

The Soul of the Earth granted its wielder a vision of whoever had fired a given projectile, affixing their image in the wielder's mind and making them easier to track.

 The legendary druid Dawan Pax used the Soul of the Earth to hunt hunters.

## Sofia Chiaroscuro

Sofia Chiaroscuro was a halfling tailor from Waterdeep who relocated to Port Nyanzaru, Chult, during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
She spoke in the third person.

History
Sofia was a Guild tailor in Waterdeep who was sent to Port Nyanzaru to study Chultan techniques and designs. She arrived in the city with materials and Northern clothing to sell, which she hoped would surprise and delight Chultan buyers. However, all of her wares were promptly confiscated by a customs assessor.While not stated directly in Neverwinter: Tomb of Annihilation, her wares were presumably confiscated because the sale of clothing in Port Nyanzaru was controlled solely by the merchant prince Zhanthi. This left her unable to do business in the city, and she subsequently sought help from the Master of the Market to acquire replacement goods.

## Soup

Soup was a wide variety of liquid cooked and cold dishes eaten across Toril, usually made with a liquid base, which could be broth, stock, water, gravy, or milk, with added vegetables, meats, and various seasonings and herbs. It was served in inns, aboard ships, and in royal castles. Sometimes, it was thickened, puréed, or clarified and it was often eaten with bread or ale. Charitable soup kitchens operated in big cities, such as Arrabar and Baldur's Gate, offering free bowls of soup to the poor and the needy.

Preparation
Perhaps the most common soup was a perpetually bubbling cauldron found in inns being cooked non-stop through the winters. They were made with roasted fowl and similar scraps added to boiling soups alongside vegetables. Fresh scraps, vegetables, and water were regularly added in as the soup boiled away for months at a time.

Varieties

Meat
Beef broth, a soup base that was often served on its own or with additional ingredients, such as a beef broth soup served with an egg and vegetables as a morningfeast at the Pampered Traveler of Waterdeep.
Boar soup, a simple game soup made with wild boar.
Carrion crawler soup, a drow dish from the Underdark.
Dragonblood soup was a dish made of a dragon's blood and various vegetables, enchanted via foodomancy.
Dragon soup, a long-simmered soup made with dragon flesh, onions, tomatoes, and milk, served at the Old Monster Shop in Waterdeep.
Frog soup was a chunky soup made by Dekanter goblins of the Greypeak Mountains.
Leek-and-hock soup was a delightful pork soup from the Boar's Head Inn in Ravens Bluff.
Leek-and-leftovers soup was a general name given to a simmered concoction of leeks with various vegetables, meats, and cheese chunks from the famed Tabra's eatery in Bargewright Inn.
Oxtail soup, a hot appetizer found in the Barony of Bloodstone in the Cold Lands as well as in Sembia.
Pea soup, a staple of the Dalelands and among travelers, available in Baldur's gate. It was a thick concoction of peas or split pea, smoked ham, and pepper.
Peppered lizard soup, a dish that dated back to Castle Felmorel in the mid–8 century DR.
Pork and cabbage soup was a famous dish made by Sot, the cook of the Laughing Goblin Inn in Phlan.
Rabbit soup, a simple game soup made with wild rabbit.
Rabbit, thyme and fennel soup, a soup found in the Moonsea region and the lands near Phlan.
Sausage and morkoth soup, a variety of everything-in perpetual soup that was cooked throughout the day with almost anything left in the kitchen tossed into it.
Smoked venison soup was a common dish made by the gnomes of Samek, on the Great Glacier. The soup was fatty and rich. It had a strong salty and smoky flavor of venison.
Snake soup, a specialty delicacy dish from the Amnian city, Crimmor.
Stagshead soup, a soup of mysterious ingredients served at Donder's Dancing Masques of Tharkar.
Venison soup, a simple game soup made with wild deer.
Welcome Soup was a robust soup from the Stargath House of Purskul made with cream, bacon, and black beans.

Poultry
Chicken with rice soup, a poultry specialty priced as six silver per bowl from The Painted Boat restaurant in Ravens Bluff.
Chicken stock was a broth served by itself or used as a soup base throughout Faerûn. Monksblade hamlet's morning soup was chicken broth-based and flavored with mushrooms.
Duck soup, a common variety of soups found in the Realms, including being a table staple from The Silver Taproom in Essembra, the Dalelands.
Turkey soup, an old recipe that dated back to the years of Athalantar.
Turkey and wildfowl soup was one of the staples made with game birds at the roadside Calling Horns inn.
Sage-and-pheasant soup was a soup served hot in Cormyr's capital, Suzail, and its Royal Castle.

Vegetable
Acorn soup a special treat from the Hog Hill inn, not far from Ravens Bluff.
Amnian Dragoon Soup was made from thinly sliced vegetables, eggs, lemon, and beef broth.
Asparagus-and-artichoke bean broth was a well-spiced dish from Kirinwood in Cormyr.
Barley soup was a simple meal from the Langdarma valley in the Utter East and Innarlith.
Blackbark soup, a dish made using a collection of forest barks, resulting in a soup as thick and meaty as beef stew.
Blalatha soup, a soup of various diced mushrooms.
Butter-tomato soup, a sweet soup made at the Sheaf of Wheat eatery in the Cormyrean village of Gaj.
Cabbage soup was yet another cheap vegetable soup found served in many places, including High Horn in Cormyr.
Carrot and ginger soup, a spiced soup from the Oak-Father's Boon of Mosstone.
Cheese-and-potato soup, a specialty of the Elfsong Tavern in Baldur's Gate.
Darblalatha soup, a soup of various diced and fried mushrooms and leeks.
Fieldgreens soup was a slow-simmered and fragrant soup found on the Sword Coast.
Fiddlehead soup, a dish prepared using fiddleheads of a fern and topped with a knob of butter, served in Baldur's Gate, the village of Dead Tree Hollow in the Vast, the Stag Steads of Cormyr, and the Moonwyvern Inn near Silverymoon.
Five-bean soup was a recipe from the land of Red Wizards – Thay.
Grass broth, a salty boiled concoction of various local greens, served on its own or with dumplings.
Leek soup, a dish made with leeks.
Mushroom soup, a common variety of soup. A highly spiced version was made by the drow of the Underdark.
Nettle soup, a type of soup cooked using the Malatran jungle nettle plant from the Malatran Plateau of Kara-Tur.
Onion soups were quite common. In the Dalelands, bowls of hot onion soup were served as morningfeast. A variety of this soup made in Vahtov, a great Zakharan city, smelled like a camel's breath.
Potato chowder, thick potato soup, flavored with leeks, garlics, onions, and milk.
Potato soup a common variety found in the Hordelands. Similar soup, naturally thickened with potato's starch, was a dish made by Sembian halflings and eaten with honeyed bread. A cold variant of the same dish was known in the village of Kirinwood in Cormyr.
Potato leek soup, another staple of Faerûn, served at the Flying Stag and the Whistling Wizard of Voonlar, Phontyr's Unicorn, in Elturel, at the Lonesome Tankard of Eveningstar in Cormyr, and favored by the dwarves, including those of Mithral Hall.
Pumpkin soup, an elvish dish made with spices and pumpkin, dating back to the ancient Myth Drannor and found across the Sword Coast.
Ratatouille was a type of northern soup made from sautéed tomatoes, onion, zucchini, and eggplant.
Vegetable broth, a soup of long-simmered vegetables, topped with a slab of tofu.
Vegetable soup made with various fresh vegetables was a simple and cheap warming meal in Innarlith, on the eastern tip of the Lake of Steam, in Impiltur, and in Ravens Bluff.
Vegetable and root sauce soup was a hearty winter dish of the Bargewright Inn.

Seafood
Blentra's oysters and wild rice in mushroom soup, a complex and delicious soup of cream, rice, oysters, and mushrooms from The Platter of Plenty in Marsember.
Brine shrimp soup, an offering from Sharkey's Bar & Grill's exotic menu, priced at five silvers per portion.
Clam chowder was a thick white soup made with clams.
Clam soup from Kozakura was cooked with mushrooms.
Cormyrian seafood soup, a variety of soup that lacked spiciness but was cooked with clams, mussels, and shrimps, spring onions, and lemon juice.
Fish-head soup, perhaps one of the cheapest seafood soups made with leftover heads of butchered fish.
Fish soups were thickened and served in tureens in Durpar.
Fish and vegetable cream soup of Yeshpek was a variety of goat's milk-base soup.
Fried eel soup was one of the staples in The Sleeping Cat of Wheloon.
Mussel-and-basil soup was a highlight of the Moonstone Mask's soup menu.
Octopus broth was more of a traditional offering of the Moonstone Mask's soup menu.
Oyster soup was made with various species of oysters, such as river-oysters that were poached in the soup, served in Crimmor.
Seafood chowder, a thick soup made with various types of seafood from The Shark Fin eatery of Ravens Bluff.
Shark fin soup was a specialty of The Shark Fin eatery of Ravens Bluff.
Spicy shrimp soup was a dish from Baldur's Gate's harborside taverns and inns.
Squash soup, a traditional summer festival vegetable soup made with chicken stock.
Stone soup, a dish from Faerûnean folklore, prepared using vegetables and sizzling hot stones.
Turtleback soup, a green and briny soup garnished with cucumber, from the city of Velen.
Turtle soup, a famous soup served at the Maided of the Moor in Mornbryn's Shield; in Neverwinter, in both the Fallen Tower and Moonstone Mask, and the Singing Sword tavern in Waterdeep.
Vegetables and fish soup was consumed by the Ffolk of the Moonshae Isles. It was a hearty broth, served hot that possessed muscle revitalizing properties.

Other
Cashew soup was a thick soup made with cashew nuts from the City of Splendors, Waterdeep.
Cheeeese soup was a special healing soup made using Luiren spring cheese by the hin healers.
Cheshleek soup, a Turmishite specialty of Khaligeira Moonthumb.
Dippin' soup, a struggle meal made from stale bread, tomatoes, and onions.
Glemmeraeve soup, a Suzailan soup made with mustard, daeradorn wine, and simmered snails in gravy.
Haendur soup, a glowworm soup flavored with sharp-flavored leaves.
Monstrous spider web soup a dish made out of boiled spiderwebs of Vaerluth in the Rauvin Mountains, presumably, only edible by goblins.
Mucjara soup, a traditional citrus soup of the Maquar people of Estagund.
Noodle soup was a staple across the nation of Shou Lung, cooked with vegetables and noodles. The best was made by Jui-jung Nu Nam in the mid–14 century DR.
Plankton soup, an offering from Sharkey's Bar & Grill's exotic menu, priced at three silvers per portion.
Zurek was a northern soup made from fermented sour flour and eggs.

History
During his many visits to the world of Earth and Ed Greenwood's home, Elminster and The Wizards Three partook in that world's versions of leek and chicken soup, cream of mushroom soup with turkey, and cream soup.

## Sound link

Sound link was a minor psionic devotion of the telepathy discipline that allowed a psionicist to connect another creature's sense of hearing to their own.

Effects
This power tapped into the auditory system of another creature from an unlimited distance away, making the manifester hear whatever the linked creature heard. In some cases, this also established a link with the target's sense of sight. If the manifestation backfired, the manifester would be deafened for 1 to 4 hours.

If the linked creature was subjected to an auditory-based ability or effect, like the song of a sirine, the manifester could also be affected.

Prerequisite Powers
In order to learn sound link a manifester first had to know the powers contact and mindlink.

Users
Sound link was found to be manifested as an innate psionic ability by emerald dragons. And while not typically a wild talent, sound link sometimes manifested as such for necromancers, who were known for developing unusual mental abilities.

Known/Notable Users
 The Watchers, illithids that served as the early warning system for the Underdark community of Nar'Vheen, included sound link among their repertoire of powers and used it to eavesdrop on distant conversations.

## Sorenhotep I

Sorenhotep I was one of the Pharaohs that reigned during the waning years of the Second Empire of Mulhorand.

History
When Pharaoh Sorenhotep I was still a young man, he was convinced that he needed a queen of beauty greater than any other mortal woman of the Realms. The Pharaoh sent messengers to all lands, even the farthest parts of the world, in search of such a wife. One of the royal messengers returned with the story of the fey princess Serenia. Without hesitation, Sorenhotep organized a caravan to the princess's land. With a Mulhorandi diplomat as its leader, it had five-thousand soldiers, one-hundred wizards, forty bards, dozens of wagons made from gold, cages full of animals from the royal zoo, and mechanical wonders from the Mulhorandi workshops—truly a delegation to behold.

But Serenia was not impressed by the wonders and lavish gifts sent her way. Many kings had the same treasures to brag about. The princess informed the Mulhorandi ambassador that only one thing could win her hand in marriage: if the king could present her the fabled unicorn of Ganathwood so the princess could ride on its back to the wedding. Sorenhotep consulted his royal magicians but was informed that no one could capture the beast; it was amazingly strong and fast, besting all hunters who had ever tried. Only a fair and pure maiden could distract the creature long enough for it to be harnessed. The Pharaoh ordered the kingdom's young maidens to be gathered and sent to Ganathwood, but months passed by, and none of the Mulhorandi girls were successful.

Princess Serenia was surprised to see the Mulhorandi diplomat return to her court with good news: the legendary unicorn was captured, but the princess had to travel to Ganathwood, where Sorenhotep kept the beast. Serenia complied and was greeted with no man, no soldier, just the forest. It did not take long for the fabled unicorn to present itself and lay its head on the princess's lap. Suddenly, the Mulhorandi soldiers leaped from their hiding and roped the unicorn.

Princess Serenia was outraged by the deception at first, but eventually came to a realization that the crafty Pharaoh did just what she asked of him and simply outwitted her. She agreed to the marriage and became Queen Serenia of Mulhorand.

## Song of King Ethos

The Song of King Ethos ballad sang about a good king who battled a powerful black dragon. In the fierce battle, the king lost his spear, lodged in the wyrm's thick hide. Desperate, Ethos called out to Torm for aid, and a dire mace materialized in his hands – the powerful enchanted ironwood mace. The king won by bringing the dire mace on the dragon's head with a satisfying crunch.

## Sorina Parnock

Sorina Parnock was a sage who specialized in the Underdark, living in Cormyr during the mid-1480s DR.

Description
Sorina had light skin and brown hair, wearing a purple and white dress.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Sorina was in town lending her expertise on Underdark matters. She got word that House Cay'Vorym was taking captured Purple Dragons and putting them in death matches at the Cay'Vorym Fighting Pit. She sent a team of adventurers down to address the matter, who infiltrated by pretending to be customers before being caught by Feryl Cay'Vorym and dropped into the pit themselves. After a fierce series of battles the arena was destroyed, Feryl was slain, and the particular threat to Purple Dragons ended.

## Soybean

The soybean was a species of legume native to the continent of Kara-Tur, widely grown for its edible bean. It was a staple crop in many parts of that continent.

Availability
In the lands of Kara-Tur, soybeans were most widely grown in the kingdoms of Shou Lung and later Kozakura, whose agrarian economy had long been dominated by rice production.The text in Kara-Tur: The Eastern Realms states that soybeans are a "new crop" in Kozakura, though "new" in relation to what period of time is not specified. It would have to be sometime before, as Swords of the Daimyo's glossary refers to tofu as a "staple food", which in turn would mean that soybeans were a staple crop of Kozakura by that time.

Over in Faerûn, soybeans began being cultivated in Marsember in the late 15 century DR, after several Shou migrated to the city and established the Shou-town district of Xiousing.

Soy Products
The soybean was very versatile in its uses, being used in a variety of different ways across Kara-Turan cuisine. They were most commonly stewed or mashed into a paste, which could then be dried, fermented, flavored, or sweetened. This paste was typically used as either a dip or a filling for buns, to make candies, or made into a salty sauce that could in turn be used to used to flavor nearly any dish. This sauce was widely known in neighboring Faerûn, to the extent that it was sold in Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue.

The other major product of soybeans was tofu, the congealed protein curds extracted from the juice or "milk" of mashed soybeans. And much like the bean it was derived from, tofu was a very versatile food item in Kara-Turan cuisine, able to be pressed into cakes or made into bean curd noodles.

## Song (sorceress)

Song, the self-described Mistress of Storms, was a mad sorceress and mercenary who worked in Cormyr in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Song had pale skin and long brown hair. She wore a simple outfit, and carried the wizard's ward to enhance her powers.

Personality

Song was extremely childish and didn't fully understand the consequences of her actions or reality in general, despite being a powerful sorceress, and titled herself the "Mistress of Storms" on a whim. She was erratic in nature and maintained a cheery and innocent mood while engaging in violence, and didn't fully understand the concept of death. While this could have been partly due to being young for an elf, it was ascribed to a form of madness that dominated her thoughts and actions.

Abilities

Though hardly a master of the elements, Song could call upon many destructive spells, including ball lightning, cyclonic blast, delayed blast fireball, disintegrate, horrid wilting, meteor swarm, and polar ray.

Relationships
Song's oldest friend was Dom, and followed his lead without really caring or understanding what was really going on.

History

Song was a longtime friend of Dom since at least one of their childhoods, and together they traveled and fought however Dom wished. They were eventually hired by Thultanthar in the mid-1480s DR to recover a piece of the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll from Cormyr, ambushing Bensen Virandson on Greenweald Lane. Despite her magical powers and many allied mercenaries, Song perished in battle, though she didn't seem to quite understand what that meant.
